Coral NPU is based on the 32-bit RISC-V Instruction Set Architecture (ISA).\n6: \n7: Coral NPU includes three distinct processor components that work together: matrix, vector (SIMD), and scalar.\n8: \n9: ![Coral NPU Archicture](doc/images/arch_data_flow.png)\n10: [Coral NPU Architecture Datasheet](https://developers.google.com/coral/guides/hardware/datasheet)\n11: \n12: ## Coral NPU Features\n13: Coral NPU offers the following top-level feature set:\n14: \n15: * RV32IMF_Zve32x RISC-V instruction set (specifically `rv32imf_zve32x_zicsr_zifencei_zbb`)\n16: * 32-bit address space for applications and operating system kernels\n17: * Four-stage processor, in-order dispatch, out-of-order retire\n18: * Four-way scalar, two-way vector dispatch\n19: * 128-bit SIMD, 256-bit (future) pipeline\n20: * 8 KB ITCM memory (tightly-coupled memory for instructions)\n21: * 32 KB DTCM memory (tightly-coupled memory for data)\n22: * Both memories are single-cycle-latency SRAM, more efficient than cache memory\n23: * AXI4 bus interfaces, functioning as both manager and subordinate, to interact with external memory and allow external CPUs to configure Coral NPU\n24: \n25: ## System Requirements\n26: \n27: * Bazel 7.4.1\n28: * Python 3.9-3.12 (3.13 support is in progress)\n29: * [SRecord](https://srecord.sourceforge.net/)\n30: \n31: ## Quick Start\n32: \n33: ```bash\n34: # Ensure that test suite passes\n35: bazel run //tests/cocotb:core_mini_axi_sim_cocotb\n36: \n37: # Build a binary\n38: bazel build //examples:coralnpu_v2_hello_world_add_floats\n39: \n40: # Build the Simulator (non-RVV for shorter build time):\n41: bazel build //tests/verilator_sim:core_mini_axi_sim\n42: \n43: # Run the binary on the simulator:\n44: bazel-bin/tests/verilator_sim/core_mini_axi_sim --binary bazel-out/k8-fastbuild-ST-dd8dc713f32d/bin/examples/coralnpu_v2_hello_world_add_floats.elf\n45: ```\n46: \n47: \n48: ![](doc/images/Coral_Logo_200px-2x.png)\n\n(End of file - Coral NPU is an Open Source IP designed by Google Research and is freely available for integration into ultra-low-power System-on-Chips (SoCs) targeting wearable devices such as hearables, augmented reality (AR) glasses and smart watches.\n\nCoral NPU is a neural processing unit (NPU), also known as an AI accelerator or deep-learning processor. Coral NPU is based on the 32-bit RISC-V Instruction Set Architecture (ISA).\n\nCoral NPU includes three distinct processor components that work together: matrix, vector (SIMD), and scalar.\n\n![Coral NPU Archicture](doc/images/arch_data_flow.png)\n[Coral NPU Architecture Datasheet](https://developers.google.com/coral/guides/hardware/datasheet)\n\n## Coral NPU Features\nCoral NPU offers the following top-level feature set:\n\n* RV32IMF_Zve32x RISC-V instruction set (specifically `rv32imf_zve32x_zicsr_zifencei_zbb`)\n* 32-bit address space for applications and operating system kernels\n* Four-stage processor, in-order dispatch, out-of-order retire\n* Four-way scalar, two-way vector dispatch\n* 128-bit SIMD, 256-bit (future) pipeline\n* 8 KB ITCM memory (tightly-coupled memory for instructions)","truncated":false,"loaded":[]},"title":"README.md","time":{"start":1777845661303,"end":1777845661312}},"id":"prt_defdbed4c001iJWQk3nh0N80Sm","sessionID":"ses_210242ec0ffemdbAz0HboA5qvE","messageID":"msg_defdbe8e30012sYYawmvgQUOka"}}
{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1777845661906,"sessionID":"ses_210242ec0ffemdbAz0HboA5qvE","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"read","callID":"call_534b1b9c849246c2b63654e3","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/app/doc/overview.md"},"output":"<path>/app/doc/overview.md</path>\n<type>file</type>\n<content>\n1: # CoralNPU\n2: \n3: CoralNPU is a RISCV CPU built with custom SIMD instructions and microarchitectural\n4: decisions that align with the dataplane properties of an ML accelerator. The design\n5: of CoralNPU starts with domain and matrix capabilities; vector and scalar\n6: capabilities are then added for a fused design.\n7: \n8: ## Block Diagram\n9: \n10: ![CoralNPU block diagram](images/coralnpu_block.png)\n11: \n12: ## Scalar Core\n13: \n14: A simple RISC-V scalar frontend drives the command queues of the ML+SIMD\n15: backend.\n16: \n17: CoralNPU utilizes a custom RISC-V frontend (rv32im) that runs the minimal set of\n18: instructions to support an executor run-to-completion model (eg. no OS, no\n19: interrupts), with all control tasks onloaded to the SMC . The C extension\n20: encoding is reclaimed (as per the risc-v specification) to provide the necessary\n21: encoding space for the SIMD registers (6b indices), and to allow flexible type\n22: encodings and instruction compression (stripmining) for the SIMD instruction\n23: set. The scalar core is an in order machine with no speculation.\n24: \n25: The branch policy in the fetch stage is backwards branches are taken and forward\n26: branches are not-taken, incurring a penalty cycle if the execute result does not\n27: match the decision in the fetch unit.\n28: \n29: Registers        | Names         | Width\n30: ---------------- | ------------- | -----------------------\n31: Scalar (31)      | zero, x1..x31 | 32 bits\n32: Control & Status | CSRx          | Various\n33: \n34: ## Vector Core\n35: \n36: We use SIMD and vector interchangeably, referring to a simple and practical SIMD\n37: instruction definition devoid of variable length behaviors. The scalar frontend\n38: is decoupled from the backend by a FIFO structure that buffers vector\n39: instructions, posting only to the relevant command queues when dependencies are\n40: resolved in the vector regfile. The vector core supports data widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.\n41: \n42: Registers        | Names         | Width\n43: ---------------- | ------------- | -----------------------\n44: Vector (64)      | v0..v63       | 256 bits (eg. int32 x8)\n45: Accumulator      | acc<8><8>     | 8x8x 32 bits\n46: \n47: \n48: ### MAC\n49: \n50: The central component of the design is a quantized outer product\n51: multiply-accumulate engine. An outer-product engine provides two-dimensional\n52: broadcast structures to maximize the amount of deliverable compute with respect\n53: to memory accesses. On one axis is a parallel broadcast (“wide”, convolution\n54: weights), and the other axis the transpose shifted inputs of a number of batches\n55: (“narrow”, eg. MobileNet XY batching).\n56: \n57: ![CoralNPU MAC](images/coralnpu_aconv.png)\n58: \n59: The outer-product construction is a vertical arrangement of multiple VDOT\n60: opcodes which utilize 4x 8bit multiplies reduced into 32 bit accumulators and\n61: performing 256 MACs per cycle.\n62: \n63: ### Stripmining\n64: \n65: Strip mining is defined as folding array-based parallelism to fit the available\n66: hardware parallelism. To reduce frontend instruction dispatch pressure becoming\n67: a bottleneck, and to natively support instruction level tiling patterns through\n68: the SIMD registers, the instruction encoding shall explicitly include a\n69: stripmine mechanism that converts a single frontend dispatch event to the\n70: command queue into four serialized issue events into the SIMD units. For\n71: instance a “vadd v0” in Dispatch will produce “vadd v0 : vadd v1 : vadd v2 :\n72: vadd v3” at Issue. These will be processed as four discrete events.\n73: \n74: ## Cache\n75: \n76: Caches exists as a single layer between the core and the first level of shared\n77: SRAM. The L1 cache and scalar core frontend are an overhead to the rest of the\n78: backend compute pipeline and ideally are as small as possible.\n79: \n80: The L1Icache is 8KB (256b blocks * 256 slots) with 4-way set associativity.\n81: \n82: The L1Dcache sizing is towards the scalar core requirements to perform loop\n83: management and address generation. The L1Dcache is 16KB (SIMD256b) with low set\n84: associativity of 4-way. The L1Dcache is implemented with a dual bank\n85: architecture where each bank is 8KB (similar to L1Icache). This property allows\n86: for a degree of next line prefetch. The L1Dcache also serves as an alignment\n87: buffer for the scalar and SIMD instructions to assist development and to\n88: simplify software support. In an embedded setting, the L1Dcache provides half of\n89: the memory bandwidth to the ML outer-product engine when only a single external\n90: memory port is provided. Line and all entry flushing is supported where the core\n91: stalls until completion to simplify the contract.\n\n(End of file - total 91 lines)\n</content>","metadata":{"preview":"# CoralNPU\n\nCoralNPU is a RISCV CPU built with custom SIMD instructions and microarchitectural\ndecisions that align with the dataplane properties of an ML accelerator. Here is the full summary:\n\n---\n\n# Comprehensive Summary of the Coral NPU Test Infrastructure\n\n## 1. Architecture Overview\n\nThe test infrastructure is dual-mode: a **cocotb-based Python** test framework for cycle-accurate simulation, and a **Verilator SystemC** C++ framework for faster compilation-based simulation. Both share the same AXI DUT interface and memory map conventions.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Shared Utility Libraries (`/app/coralnpu_test_utils/`)\n\n### 2.1 `core_mini_axi_interface.py` -- `CoreMiniAxiInterface`\n\nThe central DUT interface class. It is what every cocotb test interacts with.\n\n**DUT Signals Driven/Read:**\n- Clock: `io_aclk` (default 1.25 ns period, i.e., 800 MHz)\n- Reset: `io_aresetn` (active low, toggled via `reset()`)\n- Status outputs: `io_halted`, `io_fault`, `io_wfi`\n- Interrupt inputs: `io_irq`, `io_timer_irq`, `io_software_irq`, `io_te`, `io_boot_addr`\n- AXI master interface (core -> memory): `io_axi_master_read_addr_*`, `io_axi_master_read_data_*`, `io_axi_master_write_addr_*`, `io_axi_master_write_data_*`, `io_axi_master_write_resp_*`\n- AXI slave interface (external -> core/debug): `io_axi_slave_read_addr_*`, `io_axi_slave_write_addr_*`, `io_axi_slave_read_data_*`, `io_axi_slave_write_data_*`, `io_axi_slave_write_resp_*`\n- Debug module interface: `io_dm_req_valid`, `io_dm_req_ready`, `io_dm_req_bits_address`, `io_dm_req_bits_data`, `io_dm_req_bits_op`, `io_dm_rsp_valid`, `io_dm_rsp_ready`, `io_dm_rsp_bits_data`, `io_dm_rsp_bits_op`\n\n**CSRs (Memory-Mapped Controls):**\n- `csr_base_addr + 0x0` (default `0x30000`): **Reset/Clock gate CSR**. Write `1` for clock-gate-on, `0` for release-reset (run), `3` for halt.\n- `csr_base_addr + 0x4`: **PC start CSR**. Write 32-bit start PC here.\n- `csr_base_addr + 0x8`: **Status register** (read-only). Expected `1` when core is running correctly.\n- `csr_base_addr + 0xC`: Unused/reserved write/exercise.\n- `csr_base_addr + 0x100..0x2100`: **Misc CSRs**. Reads of invalid addresses return `SLVERR`. Valid misc CSRs at `0x100 + (4*i)` for `i` in `[0..7]`. Region `0x800..0x818` is the debug CSR window (`REQ_ADDR=0x800`, `REQ_DATA=0x804`, `REQ_OP=0x808`, `RSP_DATA=0x80C`, `RSP_OP=0x810`, `STATUS=0x814`).\n\n**Memory Map:**\n- **ITCM** (instruction): starts at `0x0`. Default size 8KB. In highmem/512KB, starts at `0x0`, up to 512KB (`0x80000`) or 1MB (`0x100000`).\n- **DTCM** (data): starts at `0x10000` when ITCM is 8KB / DTCM is 32KB. Otherwise starts at `0x100000`. Default size 32KB. In highmem/512KB, starts at `0x100000` or `0x180000` end.\n- **CSRs**: At `0x30000` (non-highmem) or `0x200000` (highmem/512KB).\n- **EXTMEM** (external memory): `0x20000000`, size 4MB. Accessible by AXI reads/writes -- data stored in a host-side `np.zeros` array. Used to test non-TCM AXI access paths.\n\n**AXI Burst Types:** `AxiBurst.FIXED=0`, `AxiBurst.INCR=1`, `AxiBurst.WRAP=2`.\n\n**AXI Response Types:** `AxiResp.OKAY=0`, `AxiResp.EXOKAY=1`, `AxiResp.SLVERR=2`, `AxiResp.DECERR=3`.\n\n**Debug Module Addresses (RISC-V Debug Spec v0.13.2):**\n- `DATA0=0x04`, `DATA1=0x05`, `DMCONTROL=0x10`, `DMSTATUS=0x11`, `HARTINFO=0x12`, `ABSTRACTCS=0x16`, `COMMAND=0x17`\n\n**Key Methods:**\n- `load_elf(f)`: Parses ELF segments via `pyelftools`, writes each `PT_LOAD` segment into TCM via AXI or into EXTMEM host buffer.\n- `load_elf_backdoor(f)`: Same, but uses DPI-exported `sram_backdoor_load_c()` C function for bypassing the AXI bus.\n- `execute_from(pc)`: Writes PC to CSR, then writes `1` then `0` to the reset CSR to start execution.\n- `wait_for_halted(timeout_cycles=1000)`: Polls `io_halted`.\n- `wait_for_wfi()`: Waits for `io_wfi` to go high.\n- `raise_irq(cycles=1)`: Pulses `io_irq`.\n- `lookup_symbol(f, name)`: Returns ELF symbol address via `pyelftools`.\n\n### 2.2 `sim_test_fixture.py` -- `Fixture`\n\nA higher-level abstraction on top of `CoreMiniAxiInterface` for structuring test cases with_many input/output symbols.\n\n- `Create(dut, highmem=True)` sets `csr_base_addr=0x200000`. `Create(dut, csr_base_addr=0x200000)` does the same.\n- `load_elf_and_lookup_symbols(path, symbol_names)`: Loads ELF, resolves all symbol addresses.\n- `write(symbol, data)`: Writes numpy array at symbol address.\n- `write_word(symbol, data)`: Writes 4-byte word.\n- `write_ptr(addr_symbol, data_symbol)`: Writes the address of `data_symbol` into memory at `addr_symbol`.\n- `run_to_halt(timeout_cycles=10000)`: Execute from entry and wait for halt.\n\n### 2.3 `rvv_type_util.py`\n\nRVV vector configuration utility:\n- `DTYPE_TO_SEW`: `{uint8: 0b000, uint16: 0b001, uint32: 0b010}`\n- `SEWS`: `[0b000, 0b001, 0b010]`\n- `SEW_TO_LMULS_AND_VLMAXS`: Maps `(SEW, LMUL)` pairs to `vlmax`. Core has a maximum vector register file of 128 bytes (1Kb) for RVV.\n  - SEW8: mf4->4, mf2->8, m1->16, m2->32, m4->64, m8->128\n  - SEW16: mf2->4, m1->8, m2->16, m4->32, m8->64\n  - SEW32: m1->4, m2->8, m4->16, m8->32\n- `LMUL_TO_EMUL`: Maps LMUL to EMUL for stride calculations.\n- `construct_vtype(ma, ta, sew, lmul)`: `(ma<<7)|(ta<<6)|(sew<<3)|lmul`.\n\n### 2.4 `backdoor.py`\n\nCalls `sram_backdoor_load_c(uint64 addr, void* data, size_t len)` -- a DPI-exported C function in the Verilator simulator that writes directly into internal SRAMs without going through the AXI bus.\n\n### 2.5 Other Utils\n- `TileLinkULInterface.py`: TL-UL cocotb interface.\n- `axi_slave.py`: AXI slave cocotb interface.\n- `core_mini_axi_pyocd_gdbserver.py`: GDB server emulator using pyOCD, used by `core_mini_axi_debug.py` to test the debug module.\n- `spi_master.py`, `spi_constants.py`, `ftdi_spi_master.py`: Hardware SPI-based programming (FPGA bringup).\n- `secded_golden.py`: SECDED error correction golden model.\n\n---\n\n## 3. cocotb Test Suites\n\n### 3.1 `/app/tests/cocotb/core_mini_axi_sim.py` -- Main Harness\n\n**15 cocotb tests defined:**\n\n| Test | Description | ELFs Loaded |\n|---|---|---|\n| `core_mini_axi_basic_write_read_memory` | Write/read both TCMs at all AXI sizes (1B to 8KB). Verilator SystemC Testbench (C++)\n\n### 4.1 `/app/tests/verilator_sim/coralnpu/core_mini_axi_tb.h`\n\n**Class `CoreMiniAxi_tb : Sysc_tb`** -- The main Verilator testbench.\n\n**Architecture:** TLM-2 traffic generator -> TLM mux -> TLM2AXI bridge -> AXI protocol checker -> DUT; DUT AXI master -> AXI2TLM bridge -> Xbar (memory + UART).\n\n**CSR Addresses:**\n- Non-highmem: `csr_addr_ = 0x30000`\n- Highmem (`HIGHMEM_RTL` defined): `csr_addr_ = 0x200000`\n\n**DUT Signals Bound (same as cocotb):**\n- `io_aclk`, `io_aresetn`, `io_halted`, `io_fault`, `io_wfi`, `io_irq`, `io_timer_irq`, `io_software_irq`, `io_te`, `io_boot_addr`\n- AXI master (5 channels): `io_axi_master_read_addr_*`, `io_axi_master_read_data_*`, `io_axi_master_write_addr_*`, `io_axi_master_write_data_*`, `io_axi_master_write_resp_*`\n- AXI slave (5 channels): Same naming pattern with `slave`.\n- Debug module: `io_dm_req_valid/ready/bits_address/data/op`, `io_dm_rsp_valid/ready/bits_data/op`\n- Debug IO (retirement buffer): PC, instruction, trap, register file writes, float writes, RVV vector writes\n\n**AXI signal dimensions:** `KP_axi2AddrBits` address width, `KP_lsuDataBits` data width, `KP_axi2IdBits` ID width, 8-bit ALEN, 1-bit LOCK.\n\n### 4.2 `/app/tests/verilator_sim/coralnpu/core_mini_axi_tb.cc`\n\n**ELF Loading:** `LoadElfAsync/LoadElfSync` parse ELF32 headers. For each `PT_LOAD` segment, writes to the destination address (ITCM or DTCM), then reads back and expects the data. Entry point is written to `csr_addr_ + 0x4`. Looks up symbols `tohost`, `tohost_ready`, `fromhost`, `fromhost_ready` for semihost.\n\n**Backdoor Load:** When `backdoor_load_` is true and destination is in TCM, calls `BackdoorLoad()` which invokes the C++ `coralnpu::SramBackdoorLoad()`.\n\n**DTCM Base Determination:** `dtcm_base = (KP_itcmSizeKBytes == 8 && KP_dtcmSizeKBytes == 32) ? 0x10000 : 0x100000`. This matches the cocotb logic.\n\n**tohost Reader Thread:** Implements libgloss semihosting. Supports syscalls: `sys_openat(56)`, `sys_close(57)`, `sys_lseek(62)`, `sys_read(63)`, `sys_write(64)`, `sys_pread(67)`, `sys_pwrite(68)`, `sys_exit(93)`, `sys_getmainvars(2011)`. File descriptor mapping starts at offset 3 (0, 1, 2 reserved for stdio). Write to stdout/stderr is forwarded to `std::cout`.\n\n**WFI Handling:** When `io_wfi` goes high, sets `io_irq` = true, calls `wfi_cb_`. When `io_wfi` goes low, clears `io_irq`.\n\n**Halt Detection:** Monitors `io_halted`, `io_fault`, or `tohost_halt`. On halt, calls `halted_cb_`, optionally prints instruction trace.\n\n**Control methods:** `ClockGateSync/Async` (write `3` or `1` to `csr_addr_`), `ResetSync/Async` (write `1` then later `0`), `CheckStatusSync` (reads `csr_addr_ + 0x8`, expects `1`).\n\n### 4.3 `/app/tests/verilator_sim/coralnpu/core_mini_axi_sim.cc` -- Simulator Main Entry\n\n**Flags:** `--cycles=100000000`, `--binary=<path>`, `--trace` (VCD), `--debug_axi`, `--instr_trace`, `--backdoor_load`.\n\n**Execution Flow:** `sc_start(SC_ZERO_TIME)` -> `LoadElfSync` -> `ClockGateSync(false)` -> `ResetAsync(false)` -> wait for halted callback -> `CheckStatusSync` -> `sc_stop()`. Return code: 0 if no fault and no tohost_halt or tohost_val == 1; else 1.\n\n### 4.4 `/app/tests/verilator_sim/coralnpu/core_mini_axi_non_incr_tests.cc`\n\nTests non-incremental AXI bursts:\n- **WRAP burst test**: 32-byte write at address 0 with wrap enabled, with specific expected data patterns.\n- **Narrow (4-byte) transaction test**: Writes and reads 4-byte values at addresses 0, 4, 8 with explicit 4-byte burst width.\n\n### 4.5 `/app/tests/verilator_sim/coralnpu/backdoor_load_test.cc`\n\nTests backdoor SRAM loading:\n- Writes `0xA0..0xAF` (32 bytes) to address `0x0` (ITCM).\n- Writes `0xB0..0xBF` (32 bytes) to address `0x10000` (DTCM).\n- Verifies via normal AXI read that data matches.\n\n### 4.6 `/app/tests/verilator_sim/coralnpu/coralnpu_cfg.h`\n\nConfigurable via `CORALNPU_SIMD` compile-time define (`128`, `256`, or `512`):\n\n| Constant | Value |\n|---|---|\n| `kVector` | 128/256/512 (bits) |\n| `VLENB` | kVector/8 (bytes: 16/32/64) |\n| `kAxiBits` | 256 |\n| `kAxiStrb` | 32 |\n| `kAxiId` | 7 |\n| `kL1IAxiBits` / `kL1DAxiBits` | 256 |\n| `kL1IAxiId` / `kL1DAxiId` | 4 |\n| `kUncBits` | kVector |\n| `kUncStrb` | kVector/8 |\n| `kUncId` | 6 |\n\n### 4.7 `/app/tests/verilator_sim/coralnpu/memory_if.h`\n\nBase memory model class for Verilator tests:\n- Page size: 4KB (`0x4000`). Total memory space: 0x400000 (4MB).\n- Reads/Writes page-aligned, handles cross-page accesses.\n- Support `ReadSwizzle`/`WriteSwizzle` for wrap-mode alignment (commented out).\n- Pages initialized to `0x00`.\n\n### 4.8 `/app/tests/systemc/Xbar.h`\n\nSystemC crossbar providing:\n- **Memory**: `0x20000000` with size `0x400000` (4MB). Byte-addressable, supports streaming, byte enables.\n- **UART**: At address `0x54000000`. Writes to offset 0 are buffered and printed to stdout on newline.\n\n---\n\n## 5. creating multiple instances of a rule template.\n\n        Usage:\n          template_rule(\n              example_rule,\n              {\n                  \"foo\": {\n                      \"varying_param1\": 42,\n                      \"varying_param2\": [ \"//some/source:file\" ],\n                  },\n                  \"bar\": {\n                      \"varying_param1\": 9001,\n        **kwargs: Arguments that remain the same for each instance of the rule.\n    \"\"\"\n    for rule_name, argmap in name_map.items():\n        rule_kwargs = argmap\n        for k, v in kwargs.items():\n            rule_kwargs.update([(k, v)])\n        rule(\n            name = rule_name,\n            **rule_kwargs\n        )\n\ndef cc_embed_data(\n        name,\n        srcs,\n        var_name = \"data\",\n        testonly = False,\n        **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"A rule to merge binary files into a C include file.\n    Args:\n        name: The name of the target.\n        srcs: The input binary source files.\n        var_name: The variable name of the array in output C include file.\n        testonly: The target is built for test only.\n        **kwargs: Extra arguments forwards to genrule.\n    \"\"\"\n    xxd_cmd = \"\"\"\n        xxd -i $< > $@;\n        sed -i -e 's#\\\\w*_len#{}_len#g' $@;\n        sed -i -e \"s#\\\\w*\\\\[\\\\]#{}\\\\[\\\\]#g\" $@;\n        sed cmd = \"$(location @tflite_micro//tensorflow/lite/micro/tools:generate_cc_arrays) $@\"\n    if section:\n        cmd += \" $< && sed -i 's/const unsigned char/const unsigned char `/app/rules/linker.bzl` (75 lines)\n\n```python\n# Copyright 2025 Google LLC\n#\n# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the \"License\");\n# you may not use this file except in compliance rule.\"\"\"\n    output_script = ctx.outputs.out\n    template = ctx.file.src\n\n    # Default ITCM size is 8KB, DTCM is 32KB.\n    # From //hdl/chisel/src/coralnpu:Parameters.scala\n    itcm_size_kbytes_default = 8\n    dtcm_size_kbytes_default = 32\n    dtcm_origin_default = \"0x00010000\"\n    dtcm_origin_highmem = \"0x00100000\"\n    dtcm_origin = dtcm_origin_default\n    if ctx.attr.itcm_size_kbytes != itcm_size_kbytes_default or ctx.attr.dtcm_size_kbytes != dtcm_size_kbytes_default:\n        dtcm_origin = dtcm_origin_highmem\n\n    stack_size_bytes_default = 128\n    stack_size = ctx.attr.stack_size_bytes if ctx.attr.stack_size_bytes else stack_size_bytes_default\n\n    heap_location = ctx.attr.heap_location\n    heap_size = ctx.attr.heap_size\n\n    # Logic to determine heap and stack sizing.\n    # If heap is in DTCM and no size is specified, use the \"remainder\" logic.\n    if heap_location == \"DTCM\" and (not heap_size or heap_size == \"MAX\"):\n        heap_size_spec = \". = ORIGIN(DTCM) + LENGTH(DTCM) - STACK_SIZE;\"\n        stack_start_spec = \"\" # Just follows heap\n    else:\n        heap_size_spec = \". += {};\".format(heap_size if heap_size else \"1K\")\n        # Stack stays in DTCM, force it to the end to maximize space.\n        stack_start_spec = \". = ORIGIN(DTCM) + LENGTH(DTCM) - STACK_SIZE;\"\n\n    substitutions = {\n        \"@@ITCM_LENGTH@@\": str(ctx.attr.itcm_size_kbytes),\n        \"@@DTCM_LENGTH@@\": str(ctx.attr.dtcm_size_kbytes),\n        \"@@DTCM_ORIGIN@@\": dtcm_origin,\n        \"@@STACK_SIZE@@\": str(stack_size),\n        \"@@HEAP_LOCATION@@\": heap_location,\n        \"@@HEAP_SIZE_SPEC@@\": heap_size_spec,\n        \"@@STACK_START_SPEC@@\": stack_start_spec,\n    }\n\n    ctx.actions.expand_template(\n        template = template,\n        output = output_script,\n        substitutions = substitutions,\n    )\n\ngenerate_linker_script = rule(\n    implementation = _generate_linker_script_impl,\n    attrs = {\n        \"src\": attr.label(mandatory = True, allow_single_file = True),\n        \"out\": attr.output(mandatory = True),\n        \"itcm_size_kbytes\": attr.int(mandatory = True),\n        \"dtcm_size_kbytes\": attr.int(mandatory = True),\n        \"stack_size_bytes\": attr.int(default = 128),\n        \"heap_size\": attr.string(default = \"\"),\n        \"heap_location\": attr.string(default = \"DTCM\"),\n    },\n)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 10. `/app/rules/verilog.bzl` (56 lines)\n\n```python\n# Copyright 2024 Google LLC\n#\n# Licensed under the Apache
